  * **Succinylcholine**: 1-1.5 mg/kg achieves paralysis within 30-40 seconds
  * **Rocuronium alternative**: 1.0-1.2 mg/kg with sugammadex 16 mg/kg backup for reversal
* **Cricoid pressure considerations** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Apply initially with force of 10 N
  * Increase to 30 N upon loss of consciousness
  * May decrease pressure if compromising laryngeal view
  * Despite controversy regarding proven efficacy, recommended for obstetric patients

### 7.9.4. ENHANCED LARYNGOSCOPY AND INTUBATION

* **Video laryngoscopy preference** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Video laryngoscopes usually provide better view than direct laryngoscopy
  * May be used preferentially for first attempt
  * Styletted and smaller-diameter cuffed endotracheal tubes (6.5 or 7.0 mm) recommended
  * Minimize tissue trauma in edematous airways
* **Confirmation and troubleshooting** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Confirm correct placement with continuous expired end-tidal capnography
  * Difficult or failed attempts should prompt following difficult airway guidelines
  * Decreasing cricoid pressure may improve view if needed

### 7.9.5. ANESTHESIA MAINTENANCE OPTIMIZATION

* **Oxygenation and ventilation** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Target adequate maternal oxygenation and normocapnia
  * FiO2 of 0.3-0.5 usually sufficient
  * Maintain normocapnia appropriate for pregnancy (PaCO2 ~30 mmHg)
* **Anesthetic depth management** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Prior to delivery: 1.0 MAC of inhaled anesthetic agents
  * After delivery: Decrease to <0.5-0.75 MAC
  * May supplement with 50-70% nitrous oxide after delivery
  * Limit dose-dependent uterine relaxation from volatile agents
* **Awareness prevention** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Patients having general anesthesia for cesarean delivery at increased risk for intraoperative recall
  * Benzodiazepines (midazolam) may be administered after delivery
  * Total intravenous anesthesia may reduce hemorrhage risk due to minimal effect on myometrial contractility

### 7.9.6. POSTOPERATIVE ANALGESIA CONSIDERATIONS

* **Regional techniques for post-operative analgesia** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Consider TAP blocks for patients who had general anesthesia
  * Wound infiltration with local anesthetic
  * Multimodal analgesia approach
  * Neuraxial opioids if conversion from regional technique

**Bottom Line:** Enhanced conduct of general anesthesia for cesarean delivery emphasizes optimal positioning with head elevation, thorough pre-oxygenation, appropriate medication dosing for rapid sequence induction, preference for video laryngoscopy, and careful maintenance balancing maternal awareness prevention with uterine contractility preservation. Postoperative regional analgesia techniques should be considered even after general anesthesia.

## 7.10. PREOPERATIVE PREPARATION, MONITORING, AND POSITIONING [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 20-21)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 7.10.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Preoperative management for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for patient care and safety [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Core obstetric anesthesia knowledge)

### 7.10.2. PREOPERATIVE ASSESSMENT [PART B]

* **History and examination**:
  * Focused assessment of airway, cardiorespiratory status [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Review of obstetric history and indications for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Assessment of bleeding risk, coagulation status [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Identification of comorbidities requiring special consideration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Laboratory studies**:
  * Not routinely required for healthy parturients [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hemoglobin, platelet count in specific situations [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Additional tests based on maternal comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.10.3. ASPIRATION PROPHYLAXIS [PART B]

* **Pharmacologic prophylaxis**:
  * H2-receptor antagonists (e.g., ranitidine) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Proton pump inhibitors (e.g., omeprazole) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Metoclopramide to promote gastric emptying [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Sodium citrate 15-30 mL (0.3 M) immediately before induction for emergency cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **NPO status**:
  * Clear liquids until 2 hours before elective cesarean [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Solid food restrictions 6-8 hours before surgery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ All pregnant women after first trimester considered to have full stomachs for emergency procedures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.10.4. MONITORING [PART B]

* **Standard ASA monitors**:
  * ECG [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Non-invasive blood pressure (1-2 minute intervals initially) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pulse oximetry [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * End-tidal CO2 (if general anesthesia) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Additional monitoring**:
  * Temperature monitoring for prolonged cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Invasive monitoring reserved for high-risk cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fetal heart rate until surgical prep begins [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.10.5. POSITIONING [PART B]

* **Uterine displacement**:
  * ⚠️ Critical to prevent aortocaval compression [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Left lateral tilt of operating table (15-20 degrees) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Alternatively, a wedge placed under right hip [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Manual uterine displacement in emergency situations [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **For neuraxial technique**:
  * Sitting or lateral position for placement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Support and careful positioning important [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.10.6. INTRAVENOUS ACCESS AND FLUID MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **IV access**:
  * At least one large-bore (16-18G) IV catheter [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Two IV lines for high-risk cases (hemorrhage, placenta accreta) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fluid management**:
  * Crystalloid prehydration or cohydration for neuraxial anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Typical volumes: 500-1000 mL crystalloid [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 💡 Coload (rapid fluid administration at time of neuraxial block) as effective as preload [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Proper preoperative preparation for cesarean delivery includes aspiration prophylaxis, appropriate monitoring, left uterine displacement to prevent aortocaval compression, and adequate intravenous access. These measures help minimize maternal-fetal risk, particularly from aspiration, hypotension, and aortocaval compression. While laboratory testing is not routine for healthy patients, comorbidities may require additional evaluation. All pregnant women after the first trimester should be considered to have full stomachs for emergency procedures.

## 7.11. NEURAXIAL ANESTHESIA [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 21-23)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 7.11.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Neuraxial techniques for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for procedure performance and patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Core obstetric anesthesia technique)

### 7.11.2. BENEFITS AND INDICATIONS [PART B]

* **Advantages of neuraxial anesthesia** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Avoiding airway manipulation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Decreasing aspiration risk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Minimizing fetal anesthetic exposure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Allowing mother to remain awake during childbirth [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Providing postoperative analgesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Techniques** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Spinal (subarachnoid block)
  * Epidural
  * Combined spinal-epidural (CSE)

### 7.11.3. SPINAL ANESTHESIA [PART B]

* **Technique**:
  * 25-27G pencil-point needle preferred to reduce PDPH risk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Typically performed at L3-L4 or L4-L5 level [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hyperbaric solution injected into CSF [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medications**:
  * Hyperbaric bupivacaine 10-12 mg (adjusted based on height, weight, age) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Adjuncts:
	* Preservative-free morphine 100-200 μg for postoperative analgesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Lipophilic opioid (fentanyl 10-25 μg or sufentanil 2.5-5 μg) for intraoperative quality [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Block characteristics**:
  * Rapid onset (3-5 minutes) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Target sensory level: T4 (T6-T4 range acceptable) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Duration: 1.5-2 hours (longer with adjuncts) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Limitations**:
  * Fixed duration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Higher risk of hypotension compared to epidural [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cannot extend if duration inadequate [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.11.4. EPIDURAL ANESTHESIA [PART B]

* **Technique**:
  * Can utilize pre-existing labor epidural catheter [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Incremental dosing to achieve surgical anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medications**:
  * 2% lidocaine with epinephrine (1:200,000) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 2-3% 2-chloroprocaine [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 0.5% bupivacaine or ropivacaine [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Adjuncts: fentanyl 50-100 μg or preservative-free morphine 2-3 mg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Block characteristics**:
  * Slower onset (15-20 minutes) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * More gradual hemodynamic changes compared to spinal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Can be extended if surgical duration prolonged [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Advantages**:
  * Titratable level and density [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Less hypotension than spinal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Can be used for postoperative analgesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.11.5. COMBINED SPINAL-EPIDURAL (CSE) [PART B]

* **Technique**:
  * Needle-through-needle or separate-needle technique [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intrathecal dose followed by epidural catheter placement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medications**:
  * Reduced intrathecal dose compared to single-shot spinal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Epidural catheter for extending duration or rescue if needed [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Advantages**:
  * Rapid onset of spinal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Flexibility of epidural catheter [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Reduced intrathecal dose may decrease hypotension risk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.11.6. COMPLICATIONS AND MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Hypotension** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Common (up to 80% without prophylaxis) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prevention: left uterine displacement, fluid loading, vasopressors [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Treatment: phenylephrine (first-line), ephedrine, additional fluid bolus [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **High or total spinal block** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Less common with appropriate dosing [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Management: airway support, ventilation, vasopressors, fluids [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Inadequate block** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Options: supplemental analgesia, epidural reinforcement, conversion to GA [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Intraoperative discomfort** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Common during peritoneal traction or exteriorization of uterus [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Management: reassurance, supplemental IV analgesics, inhaled N2O, epidural supplementation if available [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ Enhanced management based on ASA Statement 2023:
    * Acknowledge patient's discomfort and communicate that it will be managed
    * Assess nature and quality of discomfort systematically
    * Consider pausing surgical stimulation if feasible while addressing pain
    * Coordinate with surgical team to minimize uterine exteriorization when possible
    * Document patient's experience and interventions for quality improvement
* **Post-dural puncture headache** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * More common in obstetric population [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Management: conservative measures, epidural blood patch if severe or persistent [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Neuraxial anesthesia, particularly spinal anesthesia, is preferred for cesarean delivery due to safety benefits for both mother and fetus. Spinal anesthesia offers rapid onset but fixed duration, while epidural provides titratability but slower onset. Combined spinal-epidural offers advantages of both techniques. Hypotension is the most common complication and requires prophylaxis with left uterine displacement, fluids, and vasopressors. Other complications include high/total spinal, inadequate block, intraoperative discomfort, and post-dural puncture headache. The choice between techniques depends on urgency, anticipated duration, and hemodynamic considerations.

## 7.12. GENERAL ANESTHESIA [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 23-24)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 7.12.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** General anesthesia for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for airway management and patient safety [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Critical for emergency management)

### 7.12.2. INDICATIONS [PART B]

* **Maternal factors**:
  * Contraindications to neuraxial anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Patient refusal of neuraxial techniques [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Coagulopathy or anticoagulation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Infection at insertion site [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Severe hypovolemia or hemodynamic instability [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Obstetric factors**:
  * Category 1 emergency (immediate threat to maternal or fetal life) when neuraxial in not already established [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Failed neuraxial anesthesia with urgent delivery indication [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.12.3. TECHNIQUE [PART B]

* **Preparation** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Thorough airway assessment [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Difficult airway preparation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Aspiration prophylaxis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Left uterine displacement maintained [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pre-oxygenation (3-5 minutes or 8 deep breaths) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Induction** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * ⚠️ Rapid sequence induction with cricoid pressure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Induction agents: propofol 1.5-2.5 mg/kg or thiopental 4-6 mg/kg, ketamine 1-1.5 mg/kg (if hemodynamically unstable) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Muscle relaxant: succinylcholine 1-1.5 mg/kg or rocuronium 0.9-1.2 mg/kg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intubation with cuffed endotracheal tube [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Maintenance** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Volatile agent (sevoflurane, desflurane, isoflurane) at 0.5-0.75 MAC until delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 50% N2O in oxygen after delivery if desired [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Opioids typically withheld until after umbilical cord clamping [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Emergence** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Standard extubation criteria [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Extubation when fully awake with intact airway reflexes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.12.4. SPECIAL CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Awareness prevention** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Higher risk in obstetric GA due to minimizing anesthetic depth before delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ensure adequate anesthetic depth before laryngoscopy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider administering amnestic agent (e.g., midazolam) after delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Difficult airway management** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
<!-- EPA-4:K1:obstetric_difficult_airway_algorithm EPA-4:EQ3:awake_intubation_obstetric -->
  * Follow difficult airway algorithm specific to obstetrics [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider awake fiberoptic intubation if anticipated difficult airway [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Second-generation supraglottic airway can be life-saving in cannot-intubate-cannot-ventilate scenario [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal considerations** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * All anesthetic agents cross placenta [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Brief exposure usually results in minimal neonatal effects [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Longer duration of anesthesia before delivery may increase neonatal depression [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Drug transfer considerations** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] 
  * Thiopental crosses placenta rapidly, detected in umbilical vein within 30 seconds of administration to mother [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] .
  * Peak umbilical artery (fetal) levels occur within 3 minutes of maternal injection [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] .
  * No evidence that fetal outcome is affected with 'injection-to-delivery' times up to 20 minutes after maternal sleep dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] .
  * Non-depolarizing muscle relaxants generally do not cross placenta due to their large, polar nature [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] .
  * Very small amounts of succinylcholine cross placenta, usually with little effect, but may be significant if mother has enzyme deficiency [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]] .

**Bottom Line:** While neuraxial techniques are preferred for cesarean delivery, general anesthesia remains important in specific situations such as contraindications to neuraxial anesthesia, patient refusal, or extreme emergencies. Key aspects include rapid sequence induction with cricoid pressure, maintenance with volatile agents at 0.5-0.75 MAC until delivery, and careful extubation after return of airway reflexes. General anesthesia in the obstetric population presents special challenges including difficult airway management, risk of awareness, and potential fetal effects, necessitating careful preparation and vigilance.

## 7.13. POSTOPERATIVE ANALGESIA [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages[[#7.13. POSTOPERATIVE ANALGESIA [PART B]]] 24-25)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

## 7.14. QUALITY IMPROVEMENT FOR CESAREAN DELIVERY PAIN MANAGEMENT

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC #pC_SBP

ASA Statement Reference: Statement on Pain During Cesarean Delivery - Quality Improvement

### 7.14.1. IMPORTANCE OF SYSTEMATIC QUALITY IMPROVEMENT

* **Need for broader metrics** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Anesthesiologists integral to safe provision of modern obstetric care
  * Quality metrics needed to guide overall performance beyond traditional measures
  * While many hospitals track general anesthesia rates for cesarean delivery, incidence of pain associated with regional anesthesia has not often been part of quality improvement efforts and remains largely untracked

### 7.14.2. RECOMMENDED AUDIT METRICS

* **General anesthesia rates** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Percentage of patients who receive general anesthesia for cesarean delivery
  * Established quality metric for obstetric anesthesia programs
  * Should be tracked and analyzed for trends and opportunities for improvement
* **Systemic adjunct medication rates** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Percentage of patients who receive systemic analgesic/anesthetic adjunct for cesarean delivery performed under neuraxial anesthesia
  * Route, amount, and drug type tracking may reveal opportunities for quality improvement
  * May indicate potential changes in practices needed

### 7.14.3. FUTURE QUALITY IMPROVEMENT OPPORTUNITIES

* **Patient-reported outcomes** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Studies have focused on anesthesia interventions as defining event for metrics, not occurrence of self-reported pain
  * Other appropriate metrics may be considered or developed in the future
  * Patient perception and experience should be central to quality improvement efforts
* **Systematic tracking recommendations**:
  * Implement systematic tracking of patient-reported pain during cesarean delivery
  * Monitor conversion rates from neuraxial to general anesthesia
  * Track supplemental medication usage patterns
  * Analyze risk factors for inadequate anesthesia
  * Monitor patient satisfaction and follow-up outcomes

### 7.14.4. INSTITUTIONAL IMPLEMENTATION

* **Quality improvement processes** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Quality improvement processes systematically improve care and suggest opportunities for improvement
  * Should be integrated into routine obstetric anesthesia practice
  * Regular review of metrics and outcomes with multidisciplinary team
* **Benchmarking and improvement**:
  * Compare institutional metrics to published benchmarks
  * Identify areas for improvement based on data analysis
  * Implement targeted interventions to reduce pain during cesarean delivery
  * Monitor effectiveness of implemented changes

**Bottom Line:** Quality improvement for cesarean delivery pain management requires systematic tracking of both traditional metrics (general anesthesia rates) and patient-centered outcomes (pain experience, adjunct medication usage). Future efforts should focus on patient-reported outcomes rather than solely provider-defined interventions, with institutional implementation of comprehensive monitoring and improvement processes.

## 7.15. FOLLOW-UP AND REFERRAL FOR CESAREAN DELIVERY PAIN

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC #pC_SBP

ASA Statement Reference: Statement on Pain During Cesarean Delivery - Follow-Up and Referral

### 7.15.1. PATIENT ENGAGEMENT AND COMMUNICATION

* **Recognizing patient perspective** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Both anesthesiologists and obstetricians significantly underestimated when patients experienced pain during cesarean delivery in prospective studies
  * Patient experience during cesarean delivery provides unique perspective that should be engaged by clinician
  * Good communication essential to eliciting patient concerns about pain and identifying intraoperative pain

### 7.15.2. INTRAOPERATIVE RESPONSE TO PAIN

* **Immediate management** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Engage patient when they communicate having pain or discomfort during cesarean delivery
  * Acknowledge, discuss, and treat intraoperative pain appropriately
  * Communicate that pain or discomfort will be managed
  * Avoid dismissal or delayed management which may increase risk of psychological sequelae

### 7.15.3. POSTOPERATIVE FOLLOW-UP PROTOCOL

* **Systematic follow-up** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Follow-up with patients who experienced pain on postoperative visits
  * Help identify patients who experienced pain during cesarean delivery
  * Postoperative visit used for feedback and can elicit anesthetic complications
  * Improves patient satisfaction when conducted systematically

### 7.15.4. DOCUMENTATION REQUIREMENTS

* **Accurate documentation** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Appropriately document patient's stated experience of pain and actions taken to address it
  * In UK, untreated pain during cesarean delivery was most common cause of litigation related to obstetric anesthesia practice
  * Document accurate description of patient's pain or discomfort and measures taken
  * For postoperative recall of intraoperative pain, document occurrence and management

### 7.15.5. REFERRAL FOR SIGNIFICANT ADVERSE EXPERIENCES

* **Professional referral** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Refer for follow-up or consultation for significant adverse experiences to appropriate professional
  * Anesthetic complications are independent variable for development of PTSD
  * Patients who experience significant pain or discomfort may benefit from appropriate follow-up and referral
  * Long-term effects possible for patients with recall of unpleasant events during surgical procedures

**Bottom Line:** Follow-up and referral for patients experiencing pain during cesarean delivery requires systematic engagement, accurate documentation, and appropriate professional referral when indicated. Recognition that provider perception may differ from patient experience necessitates proactive follow-up to identify complications and prevent long-term psychological sequelae including PTSD.

### 7.15.6. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Anesthetic management for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **ASA Guidelines:** Evidence-based recommendations for cesarean delivery anesthesia.
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for procedural skills [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Core obstetric anesthesia topic)

### 7.15.7. INDICATIONS AND DECISION-MAKING

* **When conversion becomes necessary** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Despite best efforts, regional anesthesia failure requiring conversion occurred in 4.9% in prospective studies
  * Conversion rate for scheduled cases was 0.06% in systematic review
  * Patient preferences for conversion ideally discussed in preoperative assessment
* **Assessment process** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Acknowledge patient's experience and discomfort when increasingly uncomfortable
  * Assess nature and quality of pain and/or discomfort
  * Dismissal or delayed management may increase risk of psychological sequelae
  * Communicate that pain or discomfort will be managed

### 7.15.8. DECISION-MAKING PROTOCOL

* **Systematic approach** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Attempt to treat pain with neuraxial and/or systemic anesthetic adjuvant medication first
  * Use situational awareness and shared decision-making when deciding to convert
  * Obstetric team should be asked to pause surgery when safe to do so
  * Anesthesiologist should use knowledge and expertise in deciding when general anesthesia should be offered
  * Shared decision-making between anesthesia, surgical team, and patient when possible
  * Consider pausing surgical stimulation while intubating if feasible

### 7.15.9. PREPARATION FOR CONVERSION

* **Equipment and personnel readiness** `ASA Statement 2023`:
  * Have equipment and personnel available for general anesthesia
  * Skilled assistance should be sought for preparation of medications, airway equipment, suction
  * Assistant to provide cricoid pressure during induction and assist with airway management
  * Ensure immediate availability of difficult airway equipment

**Bottom Line:** Conversion to general anesthesia should follow systematic assessment and treatment attempts, with clear communication and shared decision-making when feasible. Despite optimal neuraxial techniques, conversion may be necessary in approximately 5% of cases, requiring immediate availability of equipment, personnel, and expertise for safe general anesthesia management.

### 7.15.10. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Postoperative pain management after cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for acute pain management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High

### 7.15.11. NEURAXIAL OPIOIDS [PART B]

* **Preservative-free morphine**:
  * Gold standard for post-cesarean analgesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Dosing:
	* Intrathecal: 100-200 μg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Epidural: 2-3 mg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Duration: 14-36 hours [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Side effects and monitoring**:
  * Pruritus (most common) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Nausea and vomiting [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ Delayed respiratory depression (rare but serious) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Monitoring requirements: hourly respiratory rate and sedation score for 12-24 hours [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.15.12. MULTIMODAL ANALGESIA [PART B]

* **Non-opioid analgesics**:
  * Scheduled acetaminophen [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * NSAIDs (ketorolac, ibuprofen) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 💡 Reduce opioid requirements by 30-50% [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Systemic opioids**:
  * Used for breakthrough pain or when neuraxial opioids contraindicated/unavailable [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * PCA may be used when necessary [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Local anesthetic techniques**:
  * Surgical site infiltration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Transversus abdominis plane (TAP) block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Quadratus lumborum block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 💡 Most effective when neuraxial morphine not used [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 7.15.13. ENHANCED RECOVERY CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Early oral intake** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Early mobilization** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Minimizing systemic opioids** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Regular administration of non-opioid analgesics** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Removal of urinary catheter within 12-24 hours** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Effective post-cesarean analgesia is best achieved with a multimodal approach, with neuraxial morphine as the foundation when neuraxial anesthesia is used. Intrathecal (100-200 μg) or epidural (2-3 mg) morphine provides up to 36 hours of analgesia but requires monitoring for respiratory depression. Supplementation with scheduled non-opioid analgesics (acetaminophen, NSAIDs) reduces opioid requirements by 30-50%. Local anesthetic techniques and systemic opioids are reserved for breakthrough pain or when neuraxial opioids are contraindicated. Enhanced recovery protocols emphasize early oral intake, mobilization, and minimizing systemic opioids.

# 8. ANESTHETIC COMPLICATIONS
<!-- EPA-7a:K7:complication_management EPA-7:S2:procedural_skills_obstetric_airway -->

> **Syllabus**: [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|Part B: Obstetrics]] | [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|Part C: Medical Knowledge]] | [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#6. Systems-Based Practice|Part C: Systems-Based Practice]]

## 8.1. PULMONARY ASPIRATION

### 8.1.1. RISK FACTORS

* **Physiologic factors**:
  * Decreased lower esophageal sphincter tone [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased intragastric pressure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Delayed gastric emptying during labor [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Clinical factors**:
  * Emergency surgery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Obesity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Recent oral intake [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pain and anxiety [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Opioid administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.1.2. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Pharmacologic prophylaxis**:
  * H2-receptor antagonists [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Proton pump inhibitors [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Metoclopramide [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Sodium citrate immediately before induction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Anesthetic technique**:
  * Neuraxial anesthesia when possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Rapid sequence induction for general anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
	* Pre-oxygenation
	* Cricoid pressure
	* Cuffed endotracheal tube
  * Awake extubation after return of protective reflexes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.1.3. MANAGEMENT OF ASPIRATION [PART B]

* **Immediate actions**:
  * Head-down position if possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Suction oropharynx [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 100% oxygen [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intubation for airway protection if not already intubated [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Subsequent management**:
  * Bronchoscopy if large particulate aspiration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ventilatory support as needed [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Antibiotics only if infection develops [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Steroids not routinely recommended [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Chest radiograph [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Arterial blood gases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Close observation for development of acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Pulmonary aspiration remains a significant risk in obstetric anesthesia due to physiological changes of pregnancy including decreased lower esophageal sphincter tone, increased intragastric pressure, and delayed gastric emptying during labor. Prevention through pharmacologic prophylaxis, appropriate NPO status, and proper anesthetic technique (preferably neuraxial) is essential. If aspiration occurs, aggressive airway management, oxygenation, and supportive care are the mainstays of treatment, with specific interventions based on the nature and severity of the aspiration.

## 8.2. HYPOTENSION [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 26-27)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.2.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Hypotension in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for prevention and management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Common and clinically significant)

### 8.2.2. ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Neuraxial anesthesia-induced**:
  * Sympathetic blockade leading to vasodilation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * More common and severe with spinal than epidural anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Can occur in up to 80% of cases without prophylaxis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Aortocaval compression**:
  * Supine positioning after 20 weeks gestation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Compression of inferior vena cava and aorta by gravid uterus [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Reduced venous return and cardiac output [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Other causes**:
  * Hypovolemia (blood loss, dehydration) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Vasovagal reaction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Anaphylaxis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * High neuraxial block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.2.3. MATERNAL AND FETAL EFFECTS [PART B]

* **Maternal**:
  * Nausea and vomiting [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Dizziness, syncope [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Decreased consciousness if severe [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal**:
  * Decreased uteroplacental perfusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fetal heart rate abnormalities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Potential fetal acidosis if prolonged and severe [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.2.4. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Positioning**:
  * Left uterine displacement (15-20 degree tilt or wedge) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid supine position [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fluid management**:
  * IV crystalloid preload or coload (500-1000 mL) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 💡 Coload (rapid fluid administration at time of block) as effective as preload [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Vasopressors**:
  * Prophylactic phenylephrine infusion (25-50 μg/min, titrated) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Alternatively, prophylactic ephedrine (5-10 mg) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Anesthetic technique**:
  * Careful dosing of local anesthetics [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Gradual onset with epidural preferred for patients with tenuous hemodynamics [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.2.5. TREATMENT [PART B]

* **First-line** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * Ensure left uterine displacement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increase IV fluid administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Phenylephrine 50-100 μg IV bolus (first-line for most cases) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ephedrine 5-10 mg IV bolus (alternative, especially if bradycardia present) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Refractory hypotension**:
  * Higher doses or repeated administration of vasopressors [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider other causes (high block, hemorrhage, anaphylaxis) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Epinephrine may be necessary in severe cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Frequent blood pressure measurements (every 1-2 minutes initially) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Continuous pulse oximetry [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fetal heart rate monitoring when feasible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Hypotension is a common complication of neuraxial anesthesia in obstetrics, potentially affecting both maternal and fetal well-being. Prevention strategies include left uterine displacement to avoid aortocaval compression, IV fluid administration, and prophylactic vasopressors (preferably phenylephrine). Treatment involves ensuring left uterine displacement, fluid administration, and vasopressors, with phenylephrine preferred in most situations. Prompt recognition and aggressive treatment are essential to prevent maternal symptoms and maintain uteroplacental perfusion.

## 8.3. TOTAL SPINAL ANESTHESIA [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 27-28)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.3.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Total spinal anesthesia in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for emergency management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Important emergency scenario)

### 8.3.2. ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Mechanisms**:
  * Excessive spread of local anesthetic in subarachnoid space [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Unintentional intrathecal injection during attempted epidural [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intrathecal migration of epidural catheter [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Subdural injection with subsequent passage to subarachnoid space [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Risk factors**:
  * Excessive local anesthetic dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Previous spinal surgery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Repeated intrathecal dosing [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.3.3. CLINICAL MANIFESTATIONS [PART B]

* **Early signs**:
  * Rapidly ascending sensory block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Progressive motor block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hypotension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Nausea, vomiting [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Late signs**:
  * Dyspnea [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Upper extremity weakness [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Difficulty speaking [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Loss of consciousness [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiac arrest (if untreated) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.3.4. MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Immediate actions**:
  * Call for help [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Airway management (100% oxygen, ventilatory support) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intubation if loss of consciousness or inadequate ventilation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Left uterine displacement maintained [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Hemodynamic support**:
  * Aggressive fluid administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Phenylephrine or ephedrine for hypotension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider epinephrine for severe hypotension or bradycardia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Atropine for symptomatic bradycardia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Supportive care**:
  * Sedation if necessary for agitation while maintaining ventilation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Reassurance if patient awake [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Continuous monitoring [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal considerations**:
  * Emergency cesarean delivery if severe fetal compromise [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Otherwise, delay delivery until maternal stability achieved [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.3.5. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Technique**:
  * Careful aspiration before epidural dosing [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Test dose to detect intrathecal or intravascular placement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Incremental epidural dosing [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Appropriate spinal dosing based on patient factors [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Assess sensory level frequently after neuraxial block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early recognition of unusually high or rapidly ascending block [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Total spinal anesthesia is a potentially life-threatening complication requiring immediate recognition and management. It can result from excessive spread of local anesthetic in the subarachnoid space, unintentional intrathecal injection, or catheter migration. The cornerstones of treatment include airway management, hemodynamic support, and left uterine displacement. Prevention through careful technique, appropriate dosing, and vigilant monitoring is essential. Maternal stabilization takes precedence over delivery unless severe fetal compromise is present.

## 8.4. LOCAL ANESTHETIC SYSTEMIC TOXICITY [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 28-29)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.4.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Local anesthetic systemic toxicity in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for prevention and emergency management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Important emergency scenario)

### 8.4.2. ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Mechanisms**:
  * Intravascular injection of local anesthetic [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Absorption of excessive amounts from tissues [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Relative overdose in vulnerable patients [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Risk factors in pregnancy**:
  * Increased cardiac output and tissue perfusion (faster absorption) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Reduced protein binding (increased free drug fraction) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Engorgement of epidural veins (increased risk of intravascular injection) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.4.3. CLINICAL MANIFESTATIONS [PART B]

* **CNS symptoms** (usually precede cardiovascular symptoms):
  * Initial excitation: perioral numbness, metallic taste, tinnitus, agitation, confusion, seizures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Later depression: unconsciousness, respiratory arrest [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cardiovascular symptoms**:
  * Initially: hypertension, tachycardia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Later: hypotension, arrhythmias, conduction blocks, myocardial depression [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Terminal: cardiovascular collapse, asystole [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Pregnancy-specific considerations**:
  * Symptoms may be masked by sedatives or general anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pregnant patients may progress more rapidly to severe toxicity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.4.4. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Technical aspects**:
  * Careful aspiration before injection [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Incremental dosing with frequent aspiration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Test dose (3 mL of local anesthetic with epinephrine 1:200,000) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Use minimum effective concentration and dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Continuous communication with patient during injection [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ECG monitoring during dosing [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.4.5. MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Initial steps**:
  * Stop local anesthetic administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Call for help [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Secure airway, administer 100% oxygen [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Treat seizures (benzodiazepines preferred) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cardiovascular support**:
  * CPR if cardiac arrest [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Standard ACLS protocols with modifications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ Small doses of epinephrine (10-100 μg) if needed [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid vasopressin, calcium channel blockers, beta blockers [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Lipid emulsion therapy**:
  * 20% lipid emulsion 1.5 mL/kg bolus [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Follow with infusion 0.25 mL/kg/min for 10 minutes after hemodynamic stability [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May repeat bolus and increase infusion rate if instability persists [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal considerations**:
  * Left uterine displacement maintained [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider emergency delivery if maternal cardiac arrest at viable gestational age [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ideally stabilize mother before delivery if possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Local anesthetic systemic toxicity is a potentially life-threatening complication with CNS effects typically preceding cardiovascular collapse. Prevention is key, including careful technique, incremental dosing, and appropriate monitoring. Pregnant patients have increased risk due to physiological changes. Management includes stopping the local anesthetic, airway support, seizure control, cardiovascular support, and lipid emulsion therapy. Maternal stabilization takes precedence over delivery unless maternal cardiac arrest occurs at viable gestation.

## 8.5. POSTDURAL PUNCTURE HEADACHE [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 29-30)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.5.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Postdural puncture headache in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for prevention and management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Common complication)

### 8.5.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND RISK FACTORS [PART B]

* **Incidence**:
  * Accidental dural puncture during epidural: 0.5-2% [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * PDPH after accidental dural puncture: 50-80% [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * PDPH after spinal with 25-27G pencil-point needle: <1% [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Risk factors**:
  * Female sex [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Young age [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pregnancy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prior history of PDPH [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Larger needle size [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cutting needle design (vs. pencil-point) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Multiple dural punctures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.5.3. CLINICAL FEATURES [PART B]

* **Timing**:
  * Typically begins 24-48 hours after dural puncture [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Rarely delayed onset up to 5-7 days [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Symptoms**:
  * Postural headache (worse when upright, better when supine) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Typically frontal and/or occipital [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May radiate to neck and shoulders [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Associated symptoms: nausea, vomiting, auditory and visual disturbances [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Differential diagnosis**:
  * Migraine [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Tension headache [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pre-eclampsia/eclampsia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Meningitis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cerebral venous thrombosis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intracranial hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.5.4. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Technical aspects**:
  * Use small-gauge pencil-point spinal needles (25-27G) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Bevel orientation parallel to dural fibers for cutting needles [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Minimize number of attempts [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Prophylactic measures**:
  * Prophylactic epidural blood patch after recognized accidental dural puncture (controversial) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intrathecal catheter placement after wet tap (may reduce PDPH incidence) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.5.5. MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Conservative treatment**:
  * Bed rest (limited evidence for efficacy) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hydration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Caffeine (oral or IV) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Analgesics (acetaminophen, NSAIDs, mild opioids) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Abdominal binder [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Epidural blood patch**:
  * Most effective treatment [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Timing: typically after 24-48 hours of conservative management [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Procedure: 15-20 mL of autologous blood injected into epidural space at or below level of dural puncture [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Success rate: 70-90% after first blood patch [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May need to repeat in 5-7% of cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Other interventions**:
  * Epidural saline, dextran, or hydroxyethyl starch (less effective than blood patch) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ACTH or cosyntropin (limited evidence) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fibrin glue or surgical closure (rare, refractory cases) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Postdural puncture headache is more common in obstetric patients and can significantly impact the postpartum experience. Prevention through appropriate needle selection (small-gauge pencil-point), correct technique, and minimizing attempts is important. Clinical presentation includes postural headache typically starting 24-48 hours after dural puncture. Conservative management is appropriate initially, but epidural blood patch remains the most effective treatment for moderate to severe cases that don't respond to conservative measures.

## 8.6. NEUROLOGIC INJURY [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Page 30)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.6.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Neurologic complications in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for risk management and patient care [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Important safety concern)

### 8.6.2. ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Anesthesia-related factors**:
  * Direct needle trauma [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Local anesthetic neurotoxicity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Epidural hematoma [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Epidural abscess [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Anterior spinal artery syndrome [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Chemical contamination [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Obstetric factors**:
  * Compression by fetal parts [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prolonged lithotomy position [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pelvic positioning during delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Forceps delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.6.3. CLINICAL MANIFESTATIONS [PART B]

* **Symptoms and signs**:
  * Persistent sensory deficit [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Motor weakness [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Abnormal reflexes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Bladder or bowel dysfunction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Specific nerves commonly affected**:
  * Lateral femoral cutaneous nerve [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Femoral nerve [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Lumbosacral trunk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Peroneal nerve [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.6.4. PREVENTION [PART B]

* **Technical aspects**:
  * Careful technique during neuraxial procedures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid paresthesias during needle placement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Appropriate patient positioning for neuraxial procedures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Patient selection**:
  * Careful evaluation of coagulation status before neuraxial procedures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consideration of risks/benefits in patients with pre-existing neurologic disease [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Intraoperative care**:
  * Proper positioning on operating table [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Padding of pressure points [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Limited time in extreme positions [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.6.5. DIAGNOSIS AND MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Evaluation**:
  * Thorough neurologic examination [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * MRI for suspected epidural hematoma or abscess [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Neurologic consultation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Treatment**:
  * Expectant management for most cases (85-90% resolve within 3 months) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Urgent surgical decompression for epidural hematoma or abscess [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Appropriate antibiotics for infectious causes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Physical therapy for persistent deficits [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Documentation and follow-up**:
  * Thorough documentation of presentation and management [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Appropriate referrals [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Regular follow-up until resolution [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Neurologic complications after obstetric anesthesia can arise from both anesthetic and obstetric factors. Most complications are temporary, but serious complications like epidural hematoma or abscess require prompt diagnosis and management. Prevention through careful technique, appropriate patient selection, and proper positioning is essential. When neurologic symptoms occur, thorough evaluation, documentation, and appropriate management are important both for patient care and medicolegal considerations.

## 8.7. MATERNAL MORTALITY [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_SBP

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Page 31)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 8.7.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Maternal mortality related to anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Systems-based practice and quality improvement [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Important safety concern)

### 8.7.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Overall maternal mortality**:
  * Leading causes worldwide: hemorrhage, hypertensive disorders, sepsis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * In developed countries, cardiovascular disease and hemorrhage predominate [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiovascular conditions responsible for over 25% of pregnancy-related deaths in the US [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Anesthesia-related mortality**:
  * Declined significantly in recent decades [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Currently accounts for <5% of maternal deaths in developed countries [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.7.3. ANESTHETIC CAUSES [PART B]

* **Historical causes**:
  * Failed airway management during general anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Aspiration pneumonitis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * High spinal/epidural anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Local anesthetic toxicity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Current causes**:
  * Complications of neuraxial anesthesia (while rare, proportionally increased as GA use has decreased) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiomyopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Severe pre-existing comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.7.4. RISK REDUCTION STRATEGIES [PART B]

* **Practice changes**:
  * Increased use of neuraxial anesthesia for cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Improved airway management techniques and devices [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Better monitoring standards [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Algorithms for management of obstetric crises [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **System improvements**:
  * Multidisciplinary teams [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Simulation training [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Emergency protocols [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maternal early warning systems [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 8.7.5. QUALITY IMPROVEMENT [PART B]

* **Maternal mortality review committees** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Confidential enquiries into maternal deaths** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Implementation of evidence-based practices** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Simulation-based team training** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Systems-based approaches to common obstetric emergencies** [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Anesthesia-related maternal mortality has significantly decreased due to increased use of neuraxial techniques, improved airway management, better monitoring, and systems-based approaches to care. Historically, failed airway management, aspiration, and high spinal/epidural anesthesia were major causes. Currently, most maternal deaths are due to underlying comorbidities or obstetric complications rather than direct anesthetic causes. Continued emphasis on quality improvement, team training, and emergency protocols is essential to further reduce maternal mortality.

# 9. MANAGEMENT OF HIGH-RISK PARTURIENTS

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 31-40)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.1.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Management of high-risk obstetric patients [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for complex patient care [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].
