## 9.2. HYPERTENSIVE DISORDERS OF PREGNANCY [PART B]
<!-- EPA-7:K5:pathophysiology_pregnancy_complications EPA-7:K6:hypertensive_disorders_pregnancy EPA-7b:EQ1:pre_eclampsia_LSCS -->

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 31-33)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.2.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Hypertensive disorders in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for management of high-risk patients [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Common and significant comorbidity)

### 9.2.2. CLASSIFICATION [PART B]

* **Chronic hypertension**: Pre-existing or diagnosed before 20 weeks gestation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Gestational hypertension**: New onset after 20 weeks without proteinuria [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Preeclampsia**: New onset hypertension after 20 weeks with end-organ involvement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]:
  * With or without severe features [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Can be superimposed on chronic hypertension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Eclampsia**: Preeclampsia with seizures not attributable to other causes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **HELLP syndrome**: Hemolysis, elevated liver enzymes, low platelets [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.2.3. PREECLAMPSIA [PART B]

* **Diagnostic criteria**:
  * BP ≥140/90 mmHg on two occasions at least 4 hours apart after 20 weeks gestation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * AND one or more of the following:
	* Proteinuria (≥300 mg/24 hours or protein/creatinine ratio ≥0.3) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Thrombocytopenia (<100,000/μL) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Renal insufficiency (creatinine >1.1 mg/dL) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Liver involvement (transaminases >2× normal) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Pulmonary edema [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Cerebral or visual symptoms [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Severe features**:
  * Systolic BP ≥160 mmHg or diastolic BP ≥110 mmHg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Thrombocytopenia <100,000/μL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Liver involvement (transaminases >2× normal, RUQ/epigastric pain) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Progressive renal insufficiency [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pulmonary edema [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cerebral or visual disturbances [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.2.4. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **General considerations**:
  * Multidisciplinary management essential [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Close monitoring of maternal and fetal status [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Blood pressure control to prevent maternal end-organ damage and placental insufficiency [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Neuraxial analgesia/anesthesia**:
  * Generally preferred if no contraindications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Benefits include:
	* Improved uteroplacental perfusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Blunting of sympathetic response to pain [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Avoiding airway manipulation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ Consider coagulation studies before neuraxial techniques if:
	* Platelet count <100,000/μL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* HELLP syndrome [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Clinical signs of coagulopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May need lower doses due to increased sensitivity to local anesthetics [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **General anesthesia considerations**:
  * Increased risk of airway edema and difficult intubation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Exaggerated hypertensive response to laryngoscopy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider pre-treatment with anti-hypertensives (labetalol, nitroglycerin) before induction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Careful hemodynamic monitoring [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.2.5. MAGNESIUM SULFATE THERAPY [PART B]

* **Indications**:
  * Seizure prophylaxis in preeclampsia with severe features [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Treatment of eclamptic seizures [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Dosing**:
  * Loading: 4-6 g IV over 15-20 minutes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maintenance: 1-2 g/hour continuous infusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Recurrent seizures: 2g bolus over 5 minutes
* **Monitoring**:
  * Clinical assessment ocontinf deep tendon reflexes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Respiratory rate (should be >12/min) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Urine output (should be >30 mL/hour) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Serum magnesium levels if toxicity suspected (therapeutic: 4-7 mEq/L) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Anesthetic implications**:
  * Potentiates neuromuscular blockade [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May enhance hypotension from neuraxial anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * ⚠️ Careful titration of neuromuscular blocking agents [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Calcium gluconate is the antidote

|Magnesium Level (mmol/L)|Clinical Effects|
|---|---|
|**0.8-1**|Normal|
|**2-4**|Therapeutic|
|**2.5-5**|Prolonged PR, widened QRS|
|**4-5**|Muscle weakness; reduced tendon reflexes, sedation|
|**>5**|Loss of tendon reflexes|
|**>7.5**|SA/AVN block, respiratory depression/arrest|
|**>12**|CHB, cardiac arrest|

- **Therapeutic range**: 2-4 mmol/L (commonly used for pre-eclampsia/eclampsia)
- **Early cardiac effects**: ECG changes (prolonged PR, widened QRS) at 2.5-5 mmol/L
- **Neurological effects**: Progressive from reduced reflexes → absent reflexes → respiratory depression
- **Critical levels**: >7.5 mmol/L associated with life-threatening complications

- **Tendon reflexes**: Important clinical marker - loss indicates dangerous levels
- **Respiratory rate**: Monitor closely as respiratory depression occurs before cardiac arrest
- **ECG monitoring**: Essential for cardiac conduction abnormalities
### 9.2.6. BLOOD PRESSURE MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Treatment thresholds**:
  * Acute severe hypertension: SBP ≥160 mmHg or DBP ≥110 mmHg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maintain BP 130-150/80-100 mmHg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medications**:
  * Labetalol: 10-20 mg IV initially, then 20-80 mg q10-20min (max 300 mg) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hydralazine: 5-10 mg IV q20min (max 20 mg) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Nifedipine: 10-20 mg PO q30min [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Sodium nitroprusside: reserved for refractory hypertension (fetal cyanide concerns) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.2.7. FLUID MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Approach**:
  * Maintain euvolemia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid aggressive fluid therapy (risk of pulmonary edema) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Careful fluid administration during neuraxial anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider invasive monitoring in severe cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Hypertensive disorders of pregnancy, particularly preeclampsia, require careful multidisciplinary management. Neuraxial techniques are generally preferred if coagulation is normal, but require careful hemodynamic management. General anesthesia presents challenges of difficult airway and exaggerated hypertensive response to laryngoscopy. Magnesium sulfate therapy for seizure prophylaxis potentiates neuromuscular blockade and may enhance hypotension from neuraxial anesthesia. Blood pressure control and appropriate fluid management are essential to prevent maternal end-organ damage while maintaining uteroplacental perfusion.

## 9.3. OBSTETRIC HEMORRHAGE [PART B]
<!-- EPA-7:K6:peripartum_haemorrhage EPA-7b:K3:pharmacology_uterotonics EPA-7b:EQ2:obstetric_haemorrhage_LSCS -->

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 33-35)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.3.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Management of obstetric hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for critical care in obstetrics [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Leading cause of maternal mortality)

### 9.3.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND CLASSIFICATION [PART B]

* **Significance**:
  * Leading cause of maternal mortality worldwide [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Major cause of maternal morbidity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Definition**: Blood loss >500 mL after vaginal delivery or >1000 mL after cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Classification**:
  * Primary (early): Within 24 hours of delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Secondary (late): 24 hours to 12 weeks postpartum [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.3.3. ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **"4 Ts" mnemonic**:
  * **Tone** (most common): Uterine atony [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * **Trauma**: Lacerations, uterine rupture, uterine inversion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * **Tissue**: Retained placenta, retained products of conception [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * **Thrombin**: Coagulopathies (congenital or acquired) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Risk factors**:
  * Previous cesarean delivery or uterine surgery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Placenta previa or accreta [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Multiple gestation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prolonged labor [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Macrosomia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Polyhydramnios [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Magnesium sulfate administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.3.4. MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Initial approach**:
  * Call for help early (obstetrician, anesthesiologist, nursing, blood bank) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Establish large-bore IV access (at least two 14-16G) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Obtain blood samples for CBC, coagulation studies, crossmatch [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Rapid crystalloid infusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider early blood product replacement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Transfusion management**:
  * Packed red blood cells for oxygen-carrying capacity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fresh frozen plasma to replace clotting factors (1:1 or 1:2 ratio with PRBCs in severe hemorrhage) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Platelets for counts <50,000/μL or dysfunctional platelets [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cryoprecipitate for fibrinogen <100-150 mg/dL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider massive transfusion protocol for severe hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Pharmacologic interventions**:
  * Uterotonics:
	* Oxytocin: First-line agent, 10-40 units in 1L crystalloid or 10 units IV bolus [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]]; Brian Hall's: 30u/500ml over 2-4H.
	* Methylergonovine: 0.25-0.5 mg IM or IV (contraindicated in hypertension) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Carboprost (15-methyl PGF2α): 0.25 mg IM q15min (max 2 mg), contraindicated in asthma [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Misoprostol: 800-1000 μg rectal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Tranexamic acid: 1 g IV over 10 minutes, repeat after 30 minutes if bleeding continues [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Recombinant Factor VIIa: Reserved for refractory hemorrhage, 90 μg/kg [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Surgical interventions**:
  * Uterine compression sutures (B-Lynch) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Uterine artery ligation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hypogastric artery ligation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Uterine tamponade (Bakri balloon) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Interventional radiology (uterine artery embolization) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Hysterectomy (last resort) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.3.5. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Neuraxial anesthesia**:
  * May be appropriate for stable patients with anticipated controlled hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider early placement if high risk for hemorrhage (allows conversion to GA if needed) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Caution in coagulopathy or severe hypotension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **General anesthesia**:
  * Indicated for hemodynamic instability, coagulopathy, patient refusal of neuraxial [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Considerations:
	* Rapid sequence induction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Careful dosing of induction agents (hemodynamic instability) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Avoid nitrous oxide in severe anemia (may expand air emboli) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Consider intraoperative cell salvage if available [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Arterial line for continuous BP monitoring and blood sampling [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Central venous access for central pressures and vasoactive drug administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider TEE or focused cardiac ultrasound in severe cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Regular lab assessments (CBC, coagulation, fibrinogen, blood gas) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fluid management**:
  * Judicious fluid resuscitation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early blood product replacement [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maintain normothermia with warming devices [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider calcium replacement with massive transfusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.3.6. PLACENTA ACCRETA SPECTRUM [PART B]

* **Definition**: Abnormal placental attachment (accreta, increta, percreta) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Risk factors**: Prior cesarean delivery, placenta previa, maternal age, prior uterine surgery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Management**:
  * Multidisciplinary planning essential [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Preoperative planning with interventional radiology possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consideration of arterial balloon catheters [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Scheduled cesarean hysterectomy may be planned [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Large-bore IV access and cross-matched blood products essential [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Obstetric hemorrhage requires prompt recognition, multidisciplinary management, and aggressive resuscitation. Anesthetic management focuses on maintaining hemodynamic stability, ensuring adequate oxygen delivery, correcting coagulopathy, and supporting surgical interventions. In stable patients with anticipated hemorrhage, neuraxial anesthesia may be appropriate with readiness to convert to general anesthesia if needed. For unstable patients or those with coagulopathy, general anesthesia with careful induction is preferred. Advanced monitoring, adequate vascular access, and early blood product replacement are essential components of care.

## 9.4. HEART DISEASE [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 35-37)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.4.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Cardiac disease in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for high-risk patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Increasing prevalence and significance)

### 9.4.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND CLASSIFICATION [PART B]

* **Significance**:
  * Cardiovascular conditions responsible for over 25% of pregnancy-related deaths in the United States [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increasing prevalence due to:
	* Women with congenital heart disease surviving to childbearing age [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Increasing maternal age and comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Peripartum cardiomyopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Classification**:
  * Congenital heart disease [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Acquired heart disease (valvular, ischemic, cardiomyopathy) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pregnancy-specific cardiac conditions (peripartum cardiomyopathy) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.4.3. PHYSIOLOGIC CHALLENGES [PART B]

* **Hemodynamic demands**:
  * Increased cardiac output (30-50%) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased heart rate (15-20%) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Decreased systemic vascular resistance (20%) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Further increases during labor and immediate postpartum period [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Specific risks**:
  * Heart failure decompensation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Arrhythmias [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Thromboembolism [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ischemia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.4.4. RISK ASSESSMENT [PART B]

* **Modified WHO classification**:
  * Class I: No increased maternal mortality, minimal morbidity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class II: Small increased mortality risk, moderate morbidity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class III: Significantly increased mortality or severe morbidity risk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class IV: Extremely high mortality risk, pregnancy contraindicated [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **High-risk lesions** (WHO Class III-IV):
  * Severe left ventricular outflow tract obstruction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Pulmonary hypertension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fontan circulation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Unrepaired cyanotic heart disease [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Systemic right ventricle [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Severe systemic ventricular dysfunction (EF <30%) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Previous peripartum cardiomyopathy with residual dysfunction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.4.5. MANAGEMENT PRINCIPLES [PART B]

* **Multidisciplinary care**:
  * Cardiology, maternal-fetal medicine, anesthesiology, critical care [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Preconception counseling when possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Detailed delivery planning [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Regular cardiac assessments throughout pregnancy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Echocardiography to assess ventricular function [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consideration of invasive monitoring for delivery in high-risk patients [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medication management**:
  * Anticoagulation management if indicated [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Continuation of essential cardiac medications (balancing maternal-fetal risks) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Modification of medications with significant teratogenic potential [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.4.6. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **General principles**:
  * Individualized approach based on specific cardiac lesion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maintain hemodynamic stability appropriate for the lesion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid excessive volume shifts [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prevent pain-induced sympathetic activation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Labor analgesia**:
  * Early epidural analgesia beneficial for most cardiac conditions [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Benefits include:
	* Reduced sympathetic activation from pain [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Decreased oxygen consumption [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Improved uteroplacental perfusion [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Availability for conversion to surgical anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Careful titration to avoid hemodynamic instability [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cesarean delivery**:
  * Neuraxial techniques preferred when possible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Single-shot spinal may cause abrupt hemodynamic changes (less ideal in critical stenotic lesions or severe ventricular dysfunction) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Slow-titrated epidural or CSE with reduced intrathecal dose preferred for better hemodynamic stability [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * General anesthesia considerations:
	* Careful induction to maintain hemodynamic targets [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Opioid-based technique may be preferred for some lesions [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Avoidance of myocardial depressants in ventricular dysfunction [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Management of pulmonary vascular resistance in pulmonary hypertension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.4.7. SPECIFIC CARDIAC LESIONS [PART B]

* **Left-sided obstructive lesions** (aortic stenosis, HOCM):
  * Maintain preload, normal to increased SVR, avoid tachycardia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Slow-titrated epidural or low-dose CSE preferred [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Phenylephrine for hypotension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Pulmonary hypertension**:
  * Very high mortality risk (30-50%) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Avoid increases in PVR (acidosis, hypoxemia, hypercarbia) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Maintain RV preload and contractility [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider pulmonary vasodilators [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early invasive monitoring [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fontan circulation**:
  * Maintain preload and avoid increases in PVR [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Slow-titrated neuraxial techniques [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * High risk of thromboembolic events [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Peripartum cardiomyopathy**:
  * Can present in last month of pregnancy or up to 5 months postpartum [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Management similar to heart failure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May require mechanical circulatory support or transplantation in severe cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Cardiac disease in pregnancy requires individualized, multidisciplinary management based on the specific lesion. Risk assessment using the modified WHO classification helps guide management decisions. Anesthetic technique must be tailored to maintain the hemodynamic profile most appropriate for the particular cardiac defect. For most lesions, slow-titrated neuraxial techniques provide better hemodynamic stability than single-shot spinal or general anesthesia. Early neuraxial analgesia for labor is beneficial to reduce sympathetic activation and decrease oxygen consumption.

## 9.5. DIABETES MELLITUS [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 37-38)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.5.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Diabetes in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for high-risk patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium

### 9.5.2. CLASSIFICATION AND PATHOPHYSIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Types**:
  * Pregestational diabetes (type 1 or type 2) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Gestational diabetes mellitus (GDM) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Physiologic changes**:
  * Insulin resistance increases during pregnancy (due to placental hormones) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Compensatory increase in insulin secretion normally occurs [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * GDM occurs when pancreatic function cannot meet increased demands [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.5.3. MATERNAL AND FETAL RISKS [PART B]

* **Maternal**:
  * Preeclampsia (2-4× increased risk) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cesarean delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Polyhydramnios [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Diabetic ketoacidosis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Worsening of diabetic retinopathy or nephropathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal**:
  * Macrosomia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Shoulder dystocia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Neonatal hypoglycemia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Congenital anomalies (in pregestational diabetes) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Intrauterine fetal death [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.5.4. MANAGEMENT DURING PREGNANCY [PART B]

* **Glycemic targets**:
  * Fasting glucose 60-95 mg/dL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 1-hour postprandial <140 mg/dL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * 2-hour postprandial <120 mg/dL [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Treatment**:
  * Diet and exercise (first-line for GDM) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Insulin (preferred pharmacologic therapy during pregnancy) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Oral hypoglycemics (metformin, glyburide) used in some cases [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Monitoring**:
  * Regular self-monitoring of blood glucose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * HbA1c monitoring (pregestational diabetes) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fetal growth assessment, particularly in third trimester [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.5.5. PERIPARTUM MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Labor and delivery**:
  * Frequent capillary glucose monitoring (hourly during active labor) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Target glucose 70-110 mg/dL during labor [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Insulin drip for pregestational diabetes or poorly controlled GDM [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Concurrent dextrose infusion to prevent hypoglycemia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Adjust insulin rates based on glucose levels [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cesarean delivery**:
  * Schedule early in morning if elective [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Administer half of usual morning insulin dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Monitor glucose levels perioperatively [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Resume regular insulin regimen postoperatively [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.5.6. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Assessment**:
  * Evaluate for end-organ involvement (neuropathy, nephropathy, cardiovascular disease) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Airway assessment (diabetic stiff joint syndrome) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Review recent glucose control [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Labor analgesia**:
  * Neuraxial techniques preferred [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early epidural placement beneficial to avoid stress-induced hyperglycemia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * No specific contraindications related to diabetes if no coagulopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cesarean anesthesia**:
  * Neuraxial techniques preferred if no contraindications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Monitor glucose throughout procedure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider stress dose steroids for patients on long-term steroid therapy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Recovery considerations**:
  * Resume glucose monitoring postoperatively [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Adjust insulin requirements (usually decreased immediately postpartum) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Monitor for hypoglycemia, especially in patients with type 1 diabetes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Diabetes in pregnancy requires careful glycemic control to optimize maternal and fetal outcomes. Peripartum management involves frequent glucose monitoring and insulin adjustment to maintain normoglycemia during labor and delivery. Anesthetic considerations include assessment for end-organ involvement, maintaining glucose control during surgery, and awareness that insulin requirements typically decrease immediately after delivery. Neuraxial anesthetic techniques are preferred and may help prevent stress-induced hyperglycemia.

## 9.6. OBESITY [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 38-39)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.6.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Obesity in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for high-risk patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** High (Increasing prevalence)

### 9.6.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND DEFINITIONS [PART B]

* **Prevalence**:
  * Increasing worldwide [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Approximately 30-40% of pregnant women in the United States [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Body Mass Index (BMI) classification**:
  * Overweight: BMI 25-29.9 kg/m² [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class I obesity: BMI 30-34.9 kg/m² [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class II obesity: BMI 35-39.9 kg/m² [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Class III obesity: BMI ≥40 kg/m² (morbid obesity) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Super obesity: BMI ≥50 kg/m² [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.6.3. COMORBIDITIES AND COMPLICATIONS [PART B]

* **Associated conditions**:
  * Hypertensive disorders of pregnancy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Gestational diabetes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Obstructive sleep apnea [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Venous thromboembolism [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiomyopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Obstetric complications**:
  * Increased cesarean delivery rate [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased failed induction of labor [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased postpartum hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased wound complications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased birth injury [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.6.4. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **General assessment**:
  * Thorough airway evaluation (increased risk of difficult intubation) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Assessment for obstructive sleep apnea [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiac evaluation for obesity-related heart disease [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Physiologic changes**:
  * Decreased functional residual capacity and increased oxygen consumption [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * More rapid desaturation during apnea [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased risk of aspiration (delayed gastric emptying, increased intra-abdominal pressure) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Altered drug pharmacokinetics (increased volume of distribution, altered clearance) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.6.5. ANESTHETIC MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Labor analgesia**:
  * Early epidural placement recommended [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Technical challenges:
	* Difficulty identifying landmarks [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Increased depth to epidural space [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Challenging positioning [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Techniques to improve success:
	* Ultrasound guidance [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Longer needles if necessary [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Optimal positioning (sitting often preferred) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Experienced provider [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Cesarean anesthesia**:
  * Neuraxial anesthesia strongly preferred when feasible [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Dosing considerations:
	* Standard dose often adequate despite increased BMI [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* May need extension of block due to longer surgical time [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * General anesthesia challenges:
	* Difficult mask ventilation and intubation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Rapid oxygen desaturation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Need for modified positioning (ramping, ear-to-sternal notch alignment) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* ⚠️ Advanced airway equipment should be immediately available [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Consider awake fiberoptic intubation for anticipated extremely difficult airway [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.6.6. PRACTICAL CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Equipment**:
  * Appropriately sized BP cuffs [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Operating table weight limits [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Specialized transfer equipment [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Longer regional needles [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Positioning**:
  * Ramping for intubation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Adequate assistance for positioning [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Prevention of pressure injuries [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Postoperative care**:
  * Increased monitoring for respiratory complications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * VTE prophylaxis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early mobilization [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider continuous pulse oximetry for patients with OSA [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Obese parturients present multiple challenges for obstetric anesthesia care, including difficult airway management, technical difficulties with neuraxial techniques, and altered physiology. Neuraxial anesthesia is strongly preferred when feasible, with early placement recommended. Preparation for potentially difficult airway management, appropriate equipment and positioning, and vigilant postoperative monitoring are essential for optimizing outcomes. Recognition and management of associated comorbidities like obstructive sleep apnea and venous thromboembolism risk are important aspects of perioperative care.

## 9.7. ADVANCED MATERNAL AGE [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Page 39)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.7.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Advanced maternal age considerations in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for high-risk patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium

### 9.7.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND DEFINITION [PART B]

* **Definition**: Typically defined as age ≥35 years at time of delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Trends**:
  * Increasing prevalence of pregnancies in this age group [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Contributes to increased comorbidity burden in obstetric population [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.7.3. ASSOCIATED RISKS [PART B]

* **Maternal**:
  * Increased prevalence of chronic hypertension [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased risk of preeclampsia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Higher rates of gestational diabetes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased placenta previa and placenta accreta [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Higher cesarean delivery rates [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased risk of peripartum cardiomyopathy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Fetal**:
  * Increased chromosomal abnormalities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Higher rates of multiple gestation (associated with fertility treatments) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased preterm birth [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased stillbirth [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.7.4. ANESTHETIC CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Preoperative assessment**:
  * Thorough evaluation for age-related comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiovascular assessment (higher prevalence of cardiac disease) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Review of medications [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Intraoperative management**:
  * Standard anesthetic techniques generally appropriate [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased vigilance for associated comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Potentially lower threshold for invasive monitoring if significant comorbidities [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Postoperative considerations**:
  * Increased risk of complications (thromboembolism, wound infection) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May require longer recovery time [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Multimodal analgesia to facilitate mobilization [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Advanced maternal age is associated with increased prevalence of comorbidities and obstetric complications, though age alone does not necessitate modification of standard anesthetic techniques. The anesthesiologist should focus on thorough assessment for and management of associated conditions, with individualized care based on specific patient factors rather than age alone. Higher rates of cesarean delivery and comorbidities such as hypertension and diabetes require careful preoperative assessment and management.

## 9.8. ANTICOAGULATION [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Pages 39-40)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.8.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Anticoagulation management in obstetric anesthesia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for high-risk patient management [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High

### 9.8.2. INDICATIONS AND AGENTS [PART B]

* **Common indications**:
  * Venous thromboembolism (VTE) prophylaxis or treatment [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Mechanical heart valves [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Atrial fibrillation [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Inherited thrombophilias [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Antiphospholipid syndrome [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Commonly used agents**:
  * Unfractionated heparin (UFH) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Low molecular weight heparin (LMWH) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Warfarin (generally avoided during pregnancy except for mechanical valves) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Direct oral anticoagulants (DOACs) - contraindicated in pregnancy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Fondaparinux (limited experience in pregnancy) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.8.3. PREGNANCY CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **Physiologic changes**:
  * Pregnancy is a hypercoagulable state [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Risk of VTE increased 4-5× in pregnancy [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Further increased risk postpartum [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Medication selection**:
  * LMWH and UFH preferred during pregnancy (do not cross placenta) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Warfarin crosses placenta and is teratogenic (embryopathy risk 6-12 weeks) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * DOACs not recommended (limited data, likely cross placenta) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.8.4. NEURAXIAL ANESTHESIA CONSIDERATIONS [PART B]

* **General principles**:
  * Risk of spinal-epidural hematoma must be balanced against benefits of neuraxial techniques [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Timing of anticoagulant administration relative to neuraxial procedure is critical [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Traumatic needle placement increases risk [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Unfractionated heparin**:
  * Prophylactic dosing:
	* Delay neuraxial procedure 4-6 hours after subcutaneous administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* No specific delay for subsequent heparin administration [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Therapeutic dosing (IV):
	* Discontinue infusion 4-6 hours before procedure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Check PTT or anti-Xa level before procedure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Delay catheter removal 4-6 hours after last dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Resume 1 hour after catheter removal [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Low molecular weight heparin**:
  * Prophylactic dosing:
	* Delay neuraxial procedure at least 12 hours after last dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Therapeutic dosing: Delay at least 24 hours [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
	* Wait at least 4 hours after procedure/catheter removal before next dose [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Warfarin**:
  * Discontinue and allow INR to normalize (≤1.5) before neuraxial procedure [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May take 3-5 days [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **DOACs**:
  * Timing varies by agent and renal function [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Generally 2-5 days washout period recommended [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.8.5. LABOR AND DELIVERY MANAGEMENT [PART B]

* **Prophylactic anticoagulation**:
  * Often held when labor begins or 12-24 hours before planned induction/cesarean [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Timing allows neuraxial technique if desired [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Therapeutic anticoagulation**:
  * Planned conversion to unfractionated heparin at 36 weeks [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Stop heparin infusion when labor begins [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Protamine available if emergency delivery needed [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Postpartum management**:
  * Restart anticoagulation 4-6 hours after vaginal delivery, 6-12 hours after cesarean [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Often at higher doses than antepartum [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Duration depends on indication and risk factors [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Anticoagulation presents a significant challenge for obstetric anesthesia, particularly regarding the safety of neuraxial techniques. Management requires careful assessment of thrombotic versus hemorrhagic risks, appropriate timing of anticoagulant discontinuation and resumption relative to neuraxial procedures, and close coordination with obstetric and hematology teams. Pregnancy is a hypercoagulable state with increased VTE risk, and anticoagulation is often required for VTE prophylaxis/treatment, mechanical heart valves, and thrombophilias. LMWH and UFH are preferred agents in pregnancy.

## 9.9. MATERNAL CARDIAC ARREST [PART B]

Tags: #pB_C1 #pC_MK #pC_PC

Part B Reference: C1 Obstetric Anesthesia (Page 40)

Part C Reference: Medical Knowledge - Clinical Applications

### 9.9.1. SYLLABUS CORRELATION

* **Part B:** Management of maternal cardiac arrest [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Part C:** Knowledge integration for critical care in obstetrics [[mmed_anaes_part_c_syllabus#1. Medical Knowledge|ⓘ]].

**Exam Weighting:** Medium-High (Critical emergency scenario)

### 9.9.2. EPIDEMIOLOGY AND ETIOLOGY [PART B]

* **Incidence**: Approximately 1 in 12,000 admissions for delivery in the United States [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Common causes**:
  * Hemorrhage [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cardiovascular disease (cardiomyopathy, myocardial infarction, aortic dissection) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Preeclampsia/eclampsia [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Amniotic fluid embolism [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Sepsis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Venous thromboembolism [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Anaphylaxis [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Medication effects/toxicity [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.9.3. PHYSIOLOGIC CHALLENGES [PART B]

* **Pregnancy-specific factors**:
  * Aortocaval compression [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Increased oxygen consumption, decreased oxygen reserve [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Difficult airway management [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Decreased chest compliance [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Altered drug distribution and effects [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.9.4. MANAGEMENT PRINCIPLES [PART B]

* **Basic life support modifications**:
  * Standard CPR with adjustments [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Left uterine displacement (manual displacement preferred during CPR) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Higher hand position for chest compressions (above gravid uterus) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Early intubation by experienced provider [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Advanced life support**:
  * Standard ACLS algorithms apply [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Consider pregnancy-specific causes [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Same medications and doses as non-pregnant patients [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Defibrillation procedures unchanged [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Perimortem cesarean delivery**:
  * Consider if no ROSC by 4 minutes of resuscitation efforts [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Ideally performed by 5 minutes (within 5 minutes of arrest) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Improves chances of maternal survival by relieving aortocaval compression [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * May permit viable fetal delivery if gestation >24 weeks [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Do not delay for fetal monitoring, ultrasound, or movement to operating room [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Continue resuscitation efforts during and after delivery [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Post-resuscitation care**:
  * Targeted temperature management may be considered [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Aggressive treatment of underlying cause [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Multidisciplinary critical care [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

### 9.9.5. TEAM PREPARATION [PART B]

* **Training**:
  * Simulation-based team training [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Clear role assignments [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Regular practice of emergency protocols [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
* **Resources**:
  * Maternal cardiac arrest cart/kit [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Perimortem cesarean delivery kit [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].
  * Cognitive aids (checklists, algorithms) [[MMed_Anaesthesiology_Part_B_Syllabus#S3 Obstetrics Anaesthesia and Analgesia|ⓘ]].

**Bottom Line:** Maternal cardiac arrest requires standard resuscitation with critical pregnancy-specific modifications, particularly left uterine displacement to relieve aortocaval compression. Perimortem cesarean delivery should be initiated promptly if resuscitation is not immediately successful, ideally within 5 minutes of arrest, to improve both maternal and fetal survival. Team preparation, clear protocols, and regular simulation training are essential for optimizing outcomes in this rare but critical emergency.
